# HKG T+24 Tmax Dataset, Attribute, Database, and Predictive-Value Audit


Generated: 2026-06-23T11:53:01.217958+00:00



## Scope and evidentiary boundary

This audit evaluates the supplied hierarchical metadata profile covering **13 dataset groups, 52 files/tables, 51 row-bearing tables, 7,219,745 profiled rows and 1,869 table attributes**. It gives a complete storage and modelling disposition for every profiled attribute.

This is a **metadata-profile audit**, not a substitute for scanning every source row or running forecast correlations. The profile provides counts, missingness, ranges, bounded categorical values and up to 100 sampled examples for free text. Those samples are sufficient to identify structure and several severe defects, but they are not a complete text vocabulary and cannot establish predictive lift. All final feature promotion still requires row-level causal validation.

The governing target is Hong Kong Observatory daily Tmax for target date T, with a decision at the defined T−24 cutoff. A field may enter a deployable feature view only if its value, issue/retrieval state and every fitted transformation were available at that cutoff.



## Executive verdict

**Preserve almost every dataset, but do not place everything in one modelling table.** The right choice is layered storage with a hard eligibility firewall:

1. **Currently suitable for production-style feature construction**
   - exact-vintage official HKO forecast data whose issue/availability precedes the cutoff;
   - causally lagged target history, after proving outcome availability;
   - deterministic calendar terms;
   - static geospatial context after reproducible derivation;
   - exact-vintage live feeds prospectively, although their current history is too short for reliable training.

2. **High scientific value but not currently deployable**
   - HKO finalized daily climate elements;
   - NOAA IGRA upper air;
   - NOAA ISD regional observations;
   - retrospective HKO tropical-cyclone best track.

3. **Future high-potential streams**
   - ARWF station forecasts;
   - decoded GFS/GEFS fields;
   - radar/satellite/nowcast;
   - live HKO station and marine feeds.

4. **Research/provenance only**
   - experiment matrices, scoreboards and OOF predictions;
   - acquisition manifests, hashes, paths and parser metadata.

The most important database design principle is therefore: **storage inclusion is not predictor eligibility**. A dataset can be worth preserving forever while being forbidden from the live feature view.

## The seven non-negotiable database rules

1. Store canonical timestamps as timezone-aware UTC; derive HKT explicitly. Never trust a column merely because its name ends in `_hkt` or `_utc`.
2. Retain separate `valid_time`, `issue_time`, `available_at`, `retrieved_at` and `ingested_at`. They are not interchangeable.
3. Every feature view must enforce `available_at <= cutoff` and must exclude target-date outcomes.
4. Keep labels and post-outcome residuals in schemas inaccessible to live inference jobs.
5. Keep raw/provenance data immutable; corrections create a new normalized version rather than rewriting history invisibly.
6. Put large binary imagery, GRIB, raster and ZIP assets in object storage; put URI, hash, size, timestamps and extraction metadata in the database.
7. Use date-effective station metadata and date-effective non-static geospatial context.

## Recommended database architecture

### `catalog`

Dataset registry, source-file registry, attribute contracts, station dimension, variable/unit vocabulary, parser versions, object URIs and profile snapshots.

### `governance`

Availability contracts, per-source cutoff rules, feature eligibility states, leakage-test outcomes, quality issues, quarantine reasons and schema versions.

### `label_core`

Canonical HKO target outcomes only. Readable by training and scoring jobs after an outcome is known; never exposed as target-T live input.

### `raw_audit` / `operational_archive_raw`

Immutable source payloads, bulletins, hashes and retrieval metadata. Full text remains intact.

### `operational_archive_normalized` / `operational_anchor`

Clean exact-vintage official forecast rows selected with deterministic issue/target/lead rules.

### `diagnostic_physics`

Finalized HKO climate, cleaned IGRA and retrospective physical/regime sources. No automatic route into live features.

### `diagnostic_station_network`

Cleaned ISD observations and summaries with date-effective station metadata. Diagnostic until operational latency is proven.

### `live_exact_vintage` / `live_nwp_anchor`

Prospectively collected feeds with exact retrieval/issue timestamps: radar summaries, lightning, tide, marine bulletins and ARWF.

### `object_catalog`

Images, radar grids, GRIBs, rasters and archives referenced by portable URI and cryptographic hash.

### `research_artifacts`, `research_metrics`, `research_oof_predictions`

Experiment specifications, matrices, diagnostics, scoreboards and OOF predictions. These schemas preserve evidence; production features are rebuilt from canonical source tables.

### `quarantine`

Rows with impossible temperatures, dates, leads, coordinates, sentinels, parser failures or time ambiguity. Quarantine is reversible and auditable; deletion is not.



## Mandatory row-level lineage columns

Every normalized meteorological or forecast row should carry, where applicable:

- `dataset_id`
- `source_file_id`
- `source_row_id`
- `source_id`
- `content_sha256`
- `valid_time_utc`
- `issue_time_utc`
- `available_at_utc`
- `retrieved_at_utc`
- `ingested_at_utc`
- `station_id` or spatial-domain key
- `quality_status`
- `eligibility_status`
- `normalization_version`
- `parser_version`
- `availability_contract_version`

Do not use a 2026 retrieval timestamp as evidence that a historical value was available operationally in 1980 or 2010.


## Detailed dataset-by-dataset assessment



## 01_hko_daily_tmax_target



### Verdict

**Include without reservation, but split its two tables by purpose.**  
`hko_daily_tmax_target_labels.parquet` is the canonical supervised label table.  
`hko_daily_extract_tmax_payload_rows.parquet` is parser/source audit material, not the canonical target and not a predictor.

### Forecasting value

This dataset is the foundation of the entire project. It supplies the realized HKO daily maximum temperature and therefore supports:

- training labels;
- official-forecast residuals;
- error-sign labels;
- high-error flags;
- causal target-memory features constructed from outcomes known before the target cutoff;
- climatology, trend, spell, persistence, volatility and analog states.

The direct value on target date **T** is forbidden at inference because it is the outcome being forecast. Its prior history is exceptionally valuable. Under the project’s strict T−24 contract, every target-memory feature must be computed only from labels whose outcomes were already known by the cutoff. The safe lag depends on the exact cutoff and publication process. A feature named `lag1` is not automatically safe merely because its date is T−1; the system must prove that the T−1 daily maximum had completed and had become available before the decision timestamp. Where this proof is not available, use the explicitly conservative lags already represented in research artifacts or establish an exact label-availability contract.

### Table-specific decision

- **Canonical labels:** load into `label_core.hko_daily_tmax`; primary key `local_date`; retain `target_tmax_c`, target station and source lineage. Apply a column firewall so this table cannot be joined into live feature views at target date T.
- **Payload rows:** retain in `raw_audit` with hashes, retrieval time, source ID, precision, completeness and parse issue. Reconcile the 169-row difference between payload rows and canonical labels, duplicate source products, and the one parse failure. Do not use payload rows directly for training labels until reconciliation is deterministic.

### Attribute guidance

- `target_tmax_c` / `absolute_daily_max_c`: critical outcome, safe only as lagged historical input.
- `local_date`: critical temporal key.
- `year`, `month`, `day`: redundant storage; derive from `local_date`.
- `value_precision`, `completeness`, `parse_issue`: quality and weighting/audit fields, not weather predictors.
- source IDs, hashes and retrieval times: indispensable lineage fields, not meteorological features.
- `availability_tier`, `operational_input_allowed`, `source_time_policy`: enforce eligibility in governance logic, never feed as ordinary numerical predictors.

### Recommended feature families

Causally rebuild persistence, 7/14/30/60/90-day slopes, curvature, rolling mean and dispersion, volatility compression/expansion, hot/cold spell duration, cumulative anomaly, reversal hazard, lag-365 analogs, decayed day-of-year climatology, climate trend, and interaction terms with official-forecast departure from target memory. These are among the safest long-history feature families in the repository.

## 02_hko_daily_climate_all_elements



### Verdict

**Include the complete long-format table in a diagnostic physics schema. Do not promote same-day values into the operational feature store until first-publication timing is proven.**

The table contains 556,399 rows and 21 physical variables across Hong Kong Observatory, King's Park, Waglan Island, North Point, Hong Kong International Airport and Hong Kong Territory. Its scientific value is high; its present point-in-time eligibility is zero because the profile explicitly identifies it as a finalized daily climate table without proven first-publication timestamps.

### Variable-by-variable value

- **Daily maximum temperature:** target-side for HKO; at other sites it can describe spatial Tmax structure diagnostically. Never use target-day HKO maximum as input.
- **Daily minimum and mean temperature:** useful for overnight heat storage, antecedent thermal state, diurnal range and cool-surge persistence. Promote only with safe lags/publication proof.
- **Mean sea-level pressure:** useful for monsoon surges, ridge/subsidence, frontal passage and tropical-cyclone environment. Level, tendency and season-conditioned interactions are high-value diagnostic signals.
- **Daily rainfall:** useful for antecedent wetness, cloud/rain persistence, soil/urban-surface cooling and suppressed solar heating. `Trace` must become a separate flag and a documented numerical convention, not a generic parse failure.
- **Mean relative humidity, wet-bulb and dew-point temperature:** strong moisture-state descriptors. Dew point and wet-bulb are usually more physically stable than relative humidity for cross-temperature comparison. Examine level, one-day change, anomaly, moisture surge and interaction with wind/pressure.
- **Mean cloud amount, bright sunshine and global solar radiation:** direct measures of radiative suppression/heating; potentially among the best mechanism variables for Tmax, but finalized target-day daily totals are inherently post-outcome and cannot be used at T−24. Use lagged persistence diagnostically or seek forecast/live substitutes.
- **Evaporation:** a broad integrated dryness/energy-balance measure; likely lower incremental value after moisture, radiation and wind but worth diagnostic testing.
- **Grass minimum temperature:** useful as a nocturnal radiative-cooling and boundary-layer stability proxy; likely season-specific.
- **Sea temperature at North Point and Waglan Island:** valuable for marine suppression, air–sea thermal contrast and sea-breeze potential. Strong diagnostic value, blocked operationally until publication timing is proven.
- **Mean wind speed and prevailing wind direction at Waglan:** useful for marine/advection regime identification. Direction must be circularly encoded and interpreted jointly with station geography.
- **Reduced visibility hours at HKIA:** possible haze, fog, moisture and stable-boundary-layer proxy; likely nonlinear and season-dependent.
- **Cloud-to-cloud and cloud-to-ground lightning:** convective-regime markers; sparse and likely more useful for high-error classification than mean point correction.

### Data-quality requirements

The profile contains 7,389 missing values, 6,916 `Trace` strings currently parsed as nonnumeric and five invalid dates. Preserve the original token, create `trace_flag`, implement variable-specific conversion, and quarantine invalid dates. Values from different units must never share an unqualified generic `value` in wide modelling code; pivot only after binding `(station_or_domain, variable, unit)` to an explicit canonical feature name.

### Best use now

Use this source as a **mechanism-discovery teacher**. Determine which physical variables explain target Tmax and official residuals, then seek T−24-safe substitutes from official text, station observations, live feeds or operational NWP. Preserve the full table because it is invaluable for physics research even though it is not currently deployable.

## 03_noaa_igra_upper_air_hkm00045004



### Verdict

**Preserve the raw pressure-level table, quarantine it, clean it, and recompute the sounding-feature table. Retain both only in diagnostic physics storage until station-specific release latency is proven.**

IGRA is scientifically one of the richest datasets in the repository: pressure, temperature, geopotential height, humidity/dew-point depression and wind from roughly 1000 to 200 hPa. Lower-tropospheric heat content, inversion structure, dryness, ridge height and vertical wind can all affect HKO Tmax. However, the current normalized values are not safe enough for direct analysis or modelling.

### Critical quality defect

The profile exposes source missing/sentinel values such as `-888.8` and `-8888` inside meteorological columns. Relative humidity reaches 1000, indicating either tenths scaling, missing codes or both. Derived fields such as `temp_850_minus_500_c` and `temp_925_minus_850_c` consequently contain physically impossible values approaching ±900. These are not rare harmless outliers; they can dominate correlations, bins and tree splits.

### Required canonical rebuild

1. Decode IGRA missing-value conventions and quality flags from the source specification.
2. Convert every sentinel to NULL before aggregation.
3. Apply the correct scale to humidity and any other fixed-width values.
4. Deduplicate period-of-record and year-to-date source overlap by `(station_id, valid_at_utc, pressure level)` with a documented source priority.
5. Recompute all level-specific fields and layer differences from cleaned rows.
6. Preserve flags and missingness indicators separately.
7. Validate UTC/HKT conversion; the profile’s identical UTC/HKT extrema are a warning.
8. Generate a quality report by year, nominal launch hour and pressure level.

### Highest-value physical attributes

- 1000/925/850-hPa temperature and geopotential height: near-surface/lower-tropospheric warm-air mass and pressure/ridge state.
- 925–850 and 850–500 temperature differences: low-level inversion/mixing and broad stability, after proper cleaning.
- Dew-point depression at 1000/925/850/700 hPa: low/mid-level dryness and cloud suppression potential.
- 850/700-hPa wind vectors: advection, monsoon and downslope/offshore regimes.
- 500-hPa height and temperature: subtropical ridge, subsidence and mid-tropospheric thermal structure.
- Vertical shear and vector turning: synoptic transition and ventilation/mixing.
- Profile completeness and key-level count: uncertainty/quality features, not direct thermodynamic signal.

### Point-in-time status

NOAA notes that some IGRA stations update near real time, but that general product statement does not prove when station HKM00045004 was available for every historical launch. The current records have valid times, not a defensible per-row issue/available-at history. Therefore IGRA remains diagnostic-only until all-row release-latency evidence or a conservative operational contract is established.

### Best use now

Mine mechanisms and convert them to safe proxies: station pressure gradients for ridge state, surface dew-point/wind for moisture/advection, official/NWP fields for low-level temperature and height, and coastal–inland contrasts for mixing/marine effects.

## 04_noaa_isd_regional_surface



### Verdict

**Preserve all raw observations, but quarantine and rebuild before deriving station features. This is probably the highest-value non-forecast information source in the repository, yet it is currently not an operationally proven vintage source.**

The 4.03 million core observations span 36 stations and include air temperature, dew point, sea-level pressure, wind speed, report type and quality codes. The station-day table provides pre-15:00 summaries. Properly repaired, this network can describe advection, inland heating, marine suppression, pressure transitions, moisture fronts and spatial disagreement.

### Critical defect: wind direction

Both `wind_direction_deg` and `wind_direction_deg_latest_before_1500` are exactly 20 degrees throughout their tables. This is impossible for a multi-decade, 36-station regional network and identifies an extraction/parser error. Reject every current directional feature and any u/v component, wind sector, upwind selection or interaction derived from it. Re-extract the direction field from the raw ISD representation and rerun all downstream station matrices.

### Station metadata defect

Raw observation coordinates include impossible values for this intended regional set, including latitude 0 and longitudes far outside South China. Use the official date-effective ISD station history as the canonical station dimension. A station can move, change name or change metadata; joining one timeless coordinate to all eras is unsafe scientifically.

### Core attributes and value

- `air_temperature_c`: very high value. Use latest-before-cutoff, morning/afternoon slopes, station-specific anomalies, ranks and pairwise spreads.
- `dew_point_c`: very high value. Use level, change, anomaly, dew-point gradient and temperature–dew-point spread.
- `sea_level_pressure_hpa`: high value where available. Use tendency, gradient, dispersion and season-conditioned regimes.
- `wind_speed_mps`: high interaction value. Use weak-wind heat buildup, ventilation, marine-flow strength and pressure/moisture interactions.
- repaired wind direction: potentially high value, especially as circular components and station-geography-aware upwind routing.
- report type and quality codes: use for filtering, source-era controls and trust/missingness; never treat arbitrary code order as a continuous number.
- observation count and latest observation time: quality/staleness/uncertainty features.
- daily minima/maxima: only usable after code-level proof that the aggregation window ends at the T−24 cutoff. The column name alone is not proof.

### Station-network features worth prioritizing

Station-specific causal anomalies, coastal–inland temperature/dew-point spreads, north–south and east–west gradients, pressure waves, rank reversals, network dispersion, group means, graph modes fitted only on prior history, station dropout patterns and propagation timing. Every normal/standardization must be station-specific and fold-local or causally updated.

### Point-in-time status

ISD is a quality-controlled archive compiled from multiple sources. Observation time does not by itself prove exact historical operational availability. Keep this source diagnostic until a conservative latency contract is established or equivalent live feeds are collected prospectively. Its role now is still extremely valuable: discovering robust station physics and designing live-safe HKO-network features.

## 05_hko_historical_rss_forecasts



### Verdict

**Include as the highest-priority operational and supervised source.** This is the core official forecast anchor and the only large source family in the profile with explicit issue, publication or availability timestamps across its usable eras.

This dataset should be split into raw exact-vintage RSS items, normalized forecast-day rows, a clean T+24 anchor table, archive-coverage metadata and research-only scored tables. The raw and normalized text must be retained in full.

### Why it is uniquely valuable

The official forecast incorporates human forecaster judgment, operational numerical guidance, radar/satellite context and local expertise not fully reproduced by the long-history diagnostic tables. It supplies:

- official Tmax and Tmin anchor;
- forecast range/midpoint;
- exact source/product era;
- issue/revision timing;
- full weather text;
- forecast staleness;
- revision momentum;
- contradiction with target memory and station state;
- labels for residual learning once actual Tmax is known;
- source-aware online residual memory and trust routing.

### Table-specific value

- **`hko_historical_rss_items.parquet`:** retain every item, title, category, GUID/link, language, feed type, description and publication/availability time. It is the raw exact-vintage corpus.
- **`hko_historical_rss_temperature_forecasts.parquet`:** canonical RSS numerical forecast-day table and primary modern official anchor.
- **`hko_press_archive_bulletins_offline.parquet`:** raw historical bulletin corpus. Retain complete text and issue metadata; quarantine rows with implausible target parsing.
- **`hko_press_archive_forecast_days.parquet`:** useful normalized historical forecast days, but current profile shows impossible maximum temperatures and lead ranges. Only explicitly scoreable, plausible and T+24-matched rows may be promoted.
- **`hko_press_archive_temperature_forecast_days.parquet`:** preferred filtered historical temperature table after enforcing `scoreable_row_valid`, lead and range checks.
- **`hko_official_t15_scored_pre2024.parquet`:** research-supervised frame containing outcomes/residuals. Keep behind a strict label firewall; it is not a live input table.
- **coverage/detail/missing files:** essential to model selection bias and archive gaps. Missing official-forecast days are not missing at random.
- **CSV twins:** do not duplicate in the database. Register them as exports; load the Parquet version.

### Mandatory quality and eligibility filters

- require issue/available time at or before the defined cutoff;
- require target date exactly matching T;
- require intended lead horizon;
- reject negative lead rows unless product semantics explicitly justify them;
- range-check temperatures;
- quarantine the observed 310°C parser result and implausible lead values;
- deduplicate repeated/revised bulletins using a deterministic vintage-selection rule;
- preserve all vintages for revision-path features;
- do not select a revision issued after the cutoff;
- use source era as an explicit state, not an accidental model artifact.

### Free-text value

Keep complete `description_text`, raw bulletin text and parsed weather/wind/temperature text. Build a versioned deterministic parser first: cloud/sun/rain/showers/thunder/fog/haze, wind direction/strength/change, temperature qualifiers, persistence/change wording, tropical-cyclone language and confidence/uncertainty cues. Text embeddings may be tested later, but must be trained/fitted fold-locally and versioned.

### Recommended production role

Start every eligible day from the official Tmax anchor, then estimate a small bounded residual correction using source-aware recent bias, target-memory state, safe station proxies and regime features. This dataset is the most direct route to current MAE improvement.

## 06_hko_tropical_cyclone_best_track



### Verdict

**Include completely in a retrospective diagnostic/regime-label schema; never use the finalized best track as an operational predictor.**

The table contains cyclone name, time, intensity, position, central pressure, maximum wind and identifiers. It is useful for explaining and clustering typhoon-related Tmax errors: cloud-shield suppression, subsidence warming, onshore/offshore flow, rainbands and post-cyclone transitions.

### Derived diagnostic features

Compute distance and bearing from HKO, storm intensity, pressure deficit, translation vector, closest approach, quadrant, time to/from closest approach, and lagged regime labels. Normalize longitude and validate storm identifiers. Use these features to:

- autopsy official-forecast residuals;
- identify whether TC days need a specialist;
- discover safe proxies in official warnings/text or operational track products;
- separate cloud/rainband days from subsidence/offshore-heating days.

### Restriction

Best-track positions and intensities are retrospective post-analysis. Even a value with valid time before T is not necessarily what forecasters knew then. Promotion requires a separate operational advisory/vintage archive; the current best-track table remains mechanism-only.

## 07_hko_radar_satellite_lightning_nowcast



### Verdict

**Include all exact-vintage metadata and derived summaries; store binary imagery in object storage. Continue collection. Do not estimate robust model value from the current one-to-three-day sample.**

The current tables include 41 rainfall-nowcast summaries, 102 lightning-count rows, 80 radar frame manifests and 4,589 satellite inventory entries. Their future value is potentially high for cloud/rain/convective suppression, but the historical sample is currently negligible.

### Component value

- **Rainfall nowcast:** useful for immediate precipitation/cloud persistence, but its stated horizon is only up to two hours and may be weak for the following day unless combined with regime persistence or official text. Validate the 544.88-mm maximum, accumulation meaning, spatial domain and fill values.
- **Lightning count:** useful as a convection flag and uncertainty scaler. All current counts are zero, so the present sample contributes no variance or estimated information gain.
- **Radar frames:** high future value if pixel data are retained. Derive domain rain fraction, echo percentiles, centroid, motion, organization, coastal approach and persistence. Manifest rows alone are not meteorological features.
- **Satellite images:** potentially valuable for cloud cover, infrared brightness temperature, cloud-top texture and motion. The current table is an inventory, not extracted image features; 132 image timestamps fail parsing and must be quarantined.

### Storage

Relational DB: source, hash, retrieval/issue/frame time, product/range, object URI, byte length and quality state. Object store: image/grid bytes. Feature store: versioned pre-cutoff numeric summaries with explicit spatial domain and extraction code version.

## 08_hko_marine_tide_coastal_waters



### Verdict

**Include and keep collecting as an exact-vintage secondary source. Current predictive evidence is insufficient and tide is likely indirect.**

### Tidal observations

Tide height may proxy coastal water state, but astronomical tide itself is unlikely to dominate next-day HKO Tmax. More promising uses are interactions with onshore wind, sea-breeze penetration, coastal boundary-layer mixing and observed-minus-predicted surge. The current 105 rows have 14.3% missing height; preserve missingness and station identity rather than broad imputation.

### South China Coastal Waters bulletin

The bulletin’s wind, weather and sea-state text can encode synoptic marine regime, showers/thunder and onshore flow. Parse direction, Beaufort/force terms, changes, convection and sea state into versioned categorical/numeric features. Only 30 rows exist, so treat it as a live feature-development stream, not a backtested source.

### Priority

Lower than official forecast, station observations and NWP, but worth retaining because it may identify marine suppression regimes not fully expressed by an area-wide Tmax forecast.

## 09_hko_arwf_station_forecasts



### Verdict

**Include as a critical live NWP-anchor collection, but do not use the present one-cycle sample to train or compare skill.**

The profile contains 530 rows, representing 53 station codes × 10 forecast dates, apparently from one model cycle. Around 28.3% of maximum/minimum forecasts are missing. Model time and last-modified fields require proper timestamp parsing.

### Why this could become exceptionally valuable

HKO states that ARWF station forecasts are direct computer output without manual adjustment and may differ from the official forecast. That makes ARWF an ideal independent expert:

- local station-level NWP temperature field;
- official-versus-model disagreement;
- station spatial gradient forecast;
- forecast spread across Hong Kong;
- model bias by station, lead, season and regime;
- trust router between human-adjusted official guidance and raw model guidance.

### Required engineering

Parse cycle/model initialization, source update/available time, valid date and lead hours. Map every station code to a station dimension. Preserve missingness by station/lead. Retain all cycles rather than only latest snapshots so revision and vintage-safe evaluation are possible. Build HKO-target and neighboring-station features only after several seasons of continuous collection.

## 10_ncep_operational_grib_inventory



### Verdict

**Include only as an object/inventory catalog until meteorological fields are decoded and cycle metadata is repaired. Future potential is extremely high.**

The table currently lists forecast hour, GFS/GEFS members, object path/length and provenance, but `cycle_date` and `cycle_hour_utc` are empty for all 3,400 rows. An inventory row is not a weather predictor.

### Required rebuild

Derive `cycle_time_utc` from filename and GRIB metadata, `valid_time_utc = cycle + forecast_hour`, product, grid, member, parameter inventory and completeness. Store GRIB bytes externally and database object URIs/hashes. Enforce availability at cutoff and retain only files actually retrieved by then.

### Highest-priority decoded fields

For a compact Hong Kong domain and T+24-relevant cycles/leads:

- 2-m temperature, dew point and relative humidity;
- 10-m u/v wind and gust;
- mean sea-level pressure and surface pressure;
- total cloud and low/mid/high cloud;
- precipitation and convective precipitation;
- downward shortwave/longwave radiation;
- boundary-layer height;
- soil moisture/temperature if demonstrably useful;
- 1000/925/850/700/500-hPa temperature, humidity, height and wind;
- ensemble mean, spread and threshold probabilities.

NCEP’s operational file naming explicitly encodes model cycle and forecast hour, so the empty fields are an ingestion defect, not an unavoidable limitation.

## 11_static_geospatial_inventory



### Verdict

**Include asset metadata in an object catalog, then derive deterministic station-context dimensions. The inventory rows themselves are not model predictors.**

### High-value derived context

For HKO and every neighboring station derive elevation, terrain slope/aspect, land–sea mask, distance/bearing to coast, coastline orientation, urban/land-use fraction, roughness/exposure, terrain barriers, pair distance/bearing/elevation difference and station group labels. These features should structure station interactions and upwind routing rather than enter as an indiscriminate feature dump.

### Temporal caution

Terrain and coastline are effectively static. Land use is not. The available 2018–2024 land-use assets cannot be silently assigned to 1950s–2000s observations. Either create date-effective land-use states, restrict them to modern-era models, or treat modern land use as a station-role annotation with an explicit limitation.

### Storage

Object store: ZIP/raster/vector assets. Database: URI, hash, CRS, resolution, license/provenance, coverage and extraction version. Station feature dimension: reproducibly derived scalar/context fields.

## 12_hkg_t24_robust_experiment_outputs



### Verdict

**Include every artifact for institutional memory, reproducibility and OOF research, but never make these matrices the canonical production feature source.**

This family contains 21 profiled tables and 1,274 profiled columns: expanded feature matrices, R14–R17 matrices, feature diagnostics, fold deltas, scoreboards and OOF predictions.

### What is valuable

- scoreboards and fold deltas preserve honest comparative evidence;
- feature diagnostics show coverage, association and failure modes;
- OOF predictions can support strictly compatible stacking and residual analyses;
- feature matrices document prior transformations and support reproduction;
- negative fold deltas reveal instability that average scores can hide.

### Why the matrices cannot be promoted wholesale

Many columns depend on timestamp-blocked HKO daily climate, IGRA or ISD values. Some inherit the IGRA sentinel contamination or ISD wind-direction defect. The same concept is duplicated across R14–R17 tables. Research artifacts also contain outcomes and labels that require a firewall.

### Safe-candidate families

The profile identifies roughly 196 target-memory/calendar-style features as candidates for causal reconstruction. Examples include lagged target values, rolling means/standard deviations, trajectories, spell states, volatility, causal climatology and harmonics. These should be recomputed from canonical target history with explicit cutoff and fold-local fitting, not copied from the artifact matrix.

### Blocked families

Upper-air, station-network and finalized daily-climate columns remain diagnostic until their source contract and quality defects are resolved. Keep their evidence because they identify physics, but do not allow them into production by inheriting a permissive flag from an old matrix.

### OOF predictions

Store model ID, fold, target date, baseline prediction, candidate prediction and outcome in `research_oof_predictions`. Stacking is allowed only when predictions are genuinely out-of-fold for every row and frames/source coverage are compatible. Never train a stacker on in-sample base predictions.

## Complete ISD station-network assessment

The station network must not be treated as 36 anonymous interchangeable columns. Each station has a physical role, coverage era, exposure and likely regime sensitivity. The dossier below is mapped against official ISD station-history metadata and includes distance and bearing from HKO.

### Recommended station tiers

- **Tier A:** immediate Hong Kong/Macao sensors with the strongest local regime interpretation.
- **Tier B:** Pearl River Delta/coastal regional sensors suitable for advection and broader regime gradients.
- **Tier C:** distant synoptic/propagation context; use with stronger shrinkage and only after demonstrating lead value.
- **Tier D:** remote or questionable context; retain for completeness but do not prioritize.

Station roles must be validated against actual date coverage and variable completeness. A station that is physically attractive but absent in a fold cannot contribute to that fold.

## Free-text attributes: preserve completely and parse twice

The JSON contains only a random sample of up to 100 strings for free-natural-text fields. That sample is a profile convenience, **not the dataset**. Store every original string with its issue time, language, bulletin/product ID, source hash and parser version.

### Pass 1: deterministic meteorological parser

Create explicit fields for:

- sunny/fine/cloudy/overcast;
- rain/showers/thunder/squalls;
- fog/mist/haze/visibility;
- hot/very hot/cool/cold;
- wind direction, force, strengthening/weakening and veering/backing;
- sea state and swell;
- tropical-cyclone language;
- timing qualifiers such as later, overnight, tomorrow morning/afternoon;
- uncertainty qualifiers;
- parser confidence and unmatched spans.

### Pass 2: learned representation

Only after a deterministic baseline exists, test fold-local bag-of-phrases, target encoding with prior-only smoothing, or embeddings. Never fit vocabulary, embeddings, scalers or text clusters on future folds. Preserve raw text so every parsed feature remains reproducible.

### Why text matters

Official weather text can identify cloud/rain and wind regimes that explain when a numerical Tmax anchor is likely too high or too low. It is more likely to add value as a residual-correction, trust or high-error feature than as a standalone direct Tmax model.



## Model-use whitelist, conditional list and hard blacklist

### Promote now after ordinary causal audits

- official forecast Tmax/Tmin/range from an eligible exact vintage;
- issue source/product/language/parser and revision state;
- full official text transformed with a versioned pre-cutoff parser;
- causal target-memory features;
- calendar/harmonic terms;
- source-specific online residual memory updated only after each outcome is known;
- deterministic static station context;
- exact-vintage live fields prospectively, once enough history exists.

### Conditional promotion

- press-archive forecast rows only after parser plausibility, lead and scoreability filters;
- ISD features only after wind repair, station metadata repair, cutoff proof and latency contract;
- IGRA only after sentinel/scale repair and release-latency proof;
- HKO daily climate only after first-publication proof or a conservative lag contract;
- ARWF only after cycle/issue/valid normalization and sufficient archived cycles;
- GFS/GEFS only after decoding and exact cycle availability;
- image/radar features only after deterministic extraction and sufficient history;
- OOF predictions only for frame-compatible, genuinely out-of-fold stacking.

### Never use as ordinary predictors

- target-T `target_tmax_c` or any post-outcome residual/error;
- finalized target-day climate fields;
- retrieval timestamps as a substitute for historical availability;
- hashes, local paths, byte sizes or export timestamps;
- all-null columns;
- current corrupt ISD wind direction;
- current uncleaned IGRA sentinel-contaminated fields;
- retrospective best-track values;
- parser-invalid temperatures/leads/dates;
- 2024+ sealed outcomes during development.



## Recommended ingestion and repair order

1. Load the profile itself into `catalog.profile_snapshot`; hash it and retain generation time.
2. Load canonical target labels and raw target audit rows; reconcile differences.
3. Load raw/normalized official forecast archives, full text and coverage tables; implement strict eligibility views.
4. Load the 36-station dimension from date-effective station history.
5. Load ISD raw rows into quarantine-capable storage; repair wind extraction and regenerate cutoff summaries.
6. Load IGRA raw rows; decode sentinels/scales and regenerate sounding features.
7. Load finalized daily climate into diagnostic storage with trace/missing/date handling.
8. Load retrospective TC tracks into diagnostic regime storage.
9. Register all radar/satellite/NWP/geospatial/archive objects; continuously append exact-vintage live metadata.
10. Normalize ARWF cycle/valid/lead/station fields and continue collection.
11. Rebuild NCEP inventory and decode a deliberately small variable/domain/lead subset.
12. Load experiment evidence and OOF outputs into research schemas.
13. Materialize only approved T−24 causal feature views.
14. Run automated leakage, range, timezone, duplicate and frame-compatibility tests before every model build.



## Acceptance tests before the database is declared model-ready

- No operational feature row lacks an `available_at_utc` or approved conservative availability contract.
- No feature has `valid_time` after the cutoff unless it is a forecast value issued before cutoff.
- UTC-to-HKT checks pass on sampled known timestamps and daylight-saving assumptions remain irrelevant for Hong Kong.
- ISD wind direction is not constant and matches raw-code samples.
- IGRA sentinels are absent from clean tables; physical ranges and layer differences pass.
- Press forecast temperatures are within the approved range and T+24 leads are exact.
- Target label duplicates and payload/label discrepancies are resolved.
- Every station joins to date-effective metadata.
- Every object URI resolves and hashes match.
- Research matrices cannot be queried by the production inference role.
- Outcome/residual columns are absent from live feature schemas.
- Confirmation-period rows remain inaccessible to development jobs.



## External source cross-checks

The source-profile decisions are consistent with official documentation:

- HKO describes daily climate products as monthly updates, while many regional/live feeds update every 5–10 minutes and official forecasts update hourly/twice daily; this supports separating finalized climate from live exact-vintage streams.
- HKO identifies regional automatic-station observations as provisional with limited validation; exact-vintage does not remove the need for quality controls.
- HKO describes ARWF station forecasts as direct computer output without manual adjustment and potentially different from official forecasts, supporting its future role as an independent expert.
- NOAA describes IGRA as pressure-level radiosonde/pilot-balloon data with temperature, height, humidity and wind; general near-real-time availability does not establish a historical station-specific release timestamp.
- NOAA describes ISD as a multi-source hourly/synoptic archive with temperature, dew point, pressure, wind and other surface elements; archive observation time is not automatically an exact operational vintage.
- NCEP’s GFS naming convention carries cycle and forecast hour, confirming that the empty cycle fields in the current inventory should be reparsed.

# Appendix B — Complete 1,869-attribute disposition

This appendix gives one explicit decision for every profiled table attribute. Repeated attribute names are intentionally retained because the same name can have a different role in a different table.

Interpretation:

- `storage_decision` answers whether/how the field should be retained.
- `db_layer` prevents accidental mixing of operational, diagnostic and research data.
- `model_role` describes its possible predictive or governance role.
- `operational_status` is the hard T−24 eligibility assessment.
- `quality_action` is required before promotion.
- `usefulness_score_0_100` rates usefulness for this HKO Tmax program, including governance value; it is not an estimated standalone feature importance.
